     - M: neighbors are F.

To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed from the graph so that the two black nodes (D and E) are completely disconnected, we need to carefully analyze the paths connecting them.

1. **Identify the black nodes:**
   - The black nodes are D and E.

2. **Find all paths from D to E:**
   - D connects to J.
   - J connects to P.
   - P connects to I.
   - I connects to C.
   - C connects to N.
   - N connects to B.
   - B connects to H.
   - H connects to G.
   - G connects to K.
   - K connects to F.
   - F connects to E.
   - This is one path: D -> J -> P -> I -> C -> N -> B -> H -> G -> K -> F -> E.

   - D connects to J.
   - J connects to P.
   - P connects to I.
   - I connects to C.
   - C connects to O.
   - O connects to Y.
   - Y connects to N.
   - N connects to B.
   - B connects to H.
   - H connects to G.
   - G connects to K.
   - K connects to F.
   - F connects to E.
   - This is another path: D -> J -> P -> I -> C -> O -> Y -> N -> B -> H -> G -> K -> F -> E.

3. **Identify the critical edges:**
   - To disconnect D and E, we need to cut all paths between them.
   - Removing the edge (D, J) will disconnect D from the rest of the graph.
   - Removing the edge (F, E) will disconnect E from the rest of the graph.

   However, we need to find the minimal set of edges. Removing just (D, J) and (F, E) is not sufficient because there are multiple paths.

4. **Minimum cut set:**
   - Removing (D, J) and (F, E) does not suffice because there are alternative paths.
   - We need to remove a set of edges that ensure no path exists between D and E.
   - Removing (J, P), (N, B), and (F, E) will ensure that all paths between D and E are cut.

Therefore, the minimum number of edges to be removed is 3.

Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(J, P), (N, B), (F, E)].

To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes, G and N, we need to identify all paths between these nodes and find the edges that, when removed, will cut off all possible routes of connection.

### Step-by-Step Analysis:

1. **Identify the two black nodes:**
   - Node G
   - Node N

2. **Examine the immediate neighboring nodes of the black nodes:**
   - Neighbors of G: X, K, F, H
   - Neighbors of N: B, Y, C

3. **Find the paths between G and N:**
   To do this, we need to explore all the connections from G to any of N's neighbors via intermediate nodes.

   - Path 1: G → F → M → L → E → B → N
   - Path 2: G → F → M → L → E → B → C → N
   - Path 3: G → F → K → J → I → C → N
   - Path 4: G → F → K → J → P → C → N
   - Path 5: G → F → K → J → D → I → C → N
   - Path 6: G → X → L → E → B → N
   - Path 7: G → X → L → E → B → C → N
   - Path 8: G → X → L → M → F → K → J → I → C → N
   - Path 9: G → X → L → M → F → K → J → P → C → N
   - Path 10: G → X → L → M → F → K → J → D → I → C → N
   - Path 11: G → H → B → N
   - Path 12: G → H → B → C → N

4. **Identify the necessary cuts:**
   To completely disconnect nodes G and N, we need to remove edges that will break all these paths. The optimal strategy here is to remove edges that appear in the maximum number of paths:
   - Removing the edge (B, C) addresses Paths 2, 3, 4, 7, 8, 9, 10, 12.
   - Removing the edge (B, E) addresses Paths 1, 2, 6, 7.
   - Removing the edge (B, N) addresses Paths 1, 2, 11, 12.

### Conclusion:

By removing the edges (B, C), (B, E), and (B, N), we ensure that all paths between nodes G and N are cut off. This forms the minimal cut set.

--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (E and J), we need to examine all possible paths between them and find the minimal cut set that disconnects these paths.

1. **Identify all paths from E to J:**
   - Path 1: E → F → G → M → L
   - Path 2: E → F → G → M → J
   - Path 3: E → H → B → C → O → P → J
   - Path 4: E → H → B → C → N → O → P → J
   - Path 5: E → H → I → P → J
   - Path 6: E → M → L → J

2. **Analyze the paths to find the minimal cut set:**
   - Removing the edge (E, F) disconnects Paths 1 and 2.
   - Removing the edge (E, H) disconnects Paths 3, 4, and 5.
   - Removing the edge (M, J) disconnects Paths 2 and 6.

However, we need to find the minimum number of edges to remove. Let's check if removing fewer edges can still disconnect E and J:

- Removing (E, F) and (M, J) without removing (E, H) still leaves Paths 3, 4, and 5 connected.
- Removing (M, J) and (E, H) without removing (E, F) still leaves Paths 1 and 2 connected.
- Removing (E, F) and (E, H) requires checking if Paths 6 still remains connected; indeed, it does through (M, J).

Thus, removing both (E, F) and (E, H) results in disconnection of all paths between E and J.

**Conclusion:**

Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(E, F), (E, H)].
